First, I need to establish my blind marks against the fixed verdicts. My blind readings give findings, OPEN items, SPLIT items. I need to compute the mark from the rules:

1. CASE DISPUTED: the situation itself, read in ordinary terms before any theory, decides a point against the fixed verdict.
2. DISAGREE: on at least one point, the theory's verdict reaches a different finding from the fixed verdict.
3. SPLIT: on at least one point, the theory's sentences support two readings that reach different findings, and the text leaves the choice open.
4. SILENT: on at least one point, the theory leaves the finding open, because it takes the deciding matter as an input the case leaves unstated, or because its sentences stop short of deciding it.
5. AGREE: on every point, the theory's verdict reaches the same finding as the fixed verdict.

My blind readings have OPEN fields and SPLIT fields. If a SPLIT exists on a point, the mark is SPLIT. If OPEN exists but the point is left open by the theory (an input the case leaves unstated or sentences stop short), the mark is SILENT. If neither and the theory reaches the same findings, AGREE.
